## Read contract (route, DTO and cursor are new)

Add `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/finding-index?domain=mini-grid-dispatch&from=<UTC>&to=<UTC>&limit=<bounded>&cursor=<opaque>` (route spelling is an Architect question below). Resolve tenant/Site via Finding access before discovery: established 401/403/404 and no foreign count. Require explicit domain and UTC half-open `[from,to)`, `from < to`, maximum 31 UTC days; typed 422 otherwise. Echo Site, UTC window and display timezone. The UI persists Site/domain/dates in its URL; date change makes a new read. Server bounds page size.

Return `schema_version`, Site/domain/window, display timezone/access basis, selection token, `knowledge_at`, `built_at`, typed status/reasons, coverage/limitations, watermarks, rows, bounded current `total_count` and `next_cursor`. Watermarks contain accepted-source marker, S3 selected assessment/run/input/configuration and analytic selection, S4 matcher/version/pass/selection. Each row has stable `(tenant_id,site_id,finding_id)` key, canonical Finding UUID, exact `finding_revision_id`, episode bounds, lifecycle/transition, selected assessment/run/model/cohort, cited accepted-evidence/configuration IDs, limitations, relation/history links and `/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}?revision_id={revision_id}`. Exact links survive reload and newer revisions; lineage comes from the selected immutable revision.

Use one transactionally consistent read or atomically published projection; `knowledge_at` is a selection cutoff, not arbitrary commit-ordered replay. **Complete discovery** means every UTC-day analytic window intersecting the request and neighbours reachable through S4's 20-minute continuity rule has S3 selection and canonical S4 match through its current selection seq on one accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis. Fence before/after read. Moving selection, affected dirty/leased/failed work, watermark mismatch, or late evidence/configuration/model change blocks `CURRENT`. A source-coverage limitation can coexist with a current assessment of available evidence, but cannot assert physical completeness.

`PENDING` means no usable completed selection; `UNASSESSED` no completed discovery basis; `STALE_PROJECTION` keeps prior rows/IDs and a reason; `FAILED` reports failed work with prior rows historical. None authorizes current count, empty/Healthy assertion or swapping stale links for a new head. A closed S3 Performance source gate returns 409 `PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED` with its reason and no rows/count/empty claim; exact pinned detail remains a separate audit route. Consume S4-05's reviewed stored gate form when merged.

One row per canonical Finding; merged-away IDs remain relationship links. Extension changes its revision; separated recurrence gets another Finding ID and link. Include closed and unconfirmed episodes. Intersect iff `episode_start < to && episode_end > from`; boundary touch is excluded. Order selected `episode_start` descending, then stable Finding UUID ascending; never by money, lifecycle or arrival. Cursor binds tenant/Site/domain/window, basis/selection token and last sort key. A changed basis invalidates it with a typed restart-first-page response; pages cannot splice revision sets. No priority tuple until a producer is reviewed.

Optional amount cell starts **unavailable**. On the stacked S5-01/LR-01 baseline, LR-01 `money_eligibility` is reviewed but S4-05 stored accepted-ID provenance is unmerged: show no current amount. After **both** reviewed contracts enter the baseline, separate review may allow amount only for the exact selected FindingRevision/window/basis and consequence head, open source, current list/consequence, eligible series, priced quantity/price and known source/display unit. Withheld/pending, mixed/unknown source, stale, wrong revision/window, unpriced or closed source means no current amount; old values use explicit audit links. No sum, savings inference or unpriced-as-zero. Money does not gate row/empty delivery.

# Architect answer - S4-04 index contract (2026-10-15)

Authority: `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index` and `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice`. These rulings settle the three API choices in `tasks/S4-04-site-finding-index.md`; they do not approve UI copy or change the Finding detail route.

1. **Route and cursor error - accept.** Add `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/finding-index` with the packet's required query and scope. Leave `/findings/{finding_id}` and the consumed `findings_over` helper unchanged. When a cursor's bound selection token or scope differs from the current fenced basis, return HTTP 409 `INDEX_BASIS_CHANGED` with a machine-readable reason and instruction to request page one without a cursor. Return no rows, count or replacement cursor in that response. A malformed cursor remains a typed 422; do not treat it as a basis change.
2. **Historical rows - accept.** Return `STALE_PROJECTION` or `FAILED` rows only from a durable prior selected snapshot containing the exact canonical Finding and FindingRevision IDs, sort order and basis used for that response. Recheck scope before reading it and label its rows and count historical, never current. If no such snapshot exists, return `PENDING` or `UNASSESSED` with no rows and no current count as applicable; `FAILED` may report the failure with no historical rows. Never reconstruct historical rows from latest Finding heads. A closed Performance source gate still returns its existing 409 with no index rows or count.
3. **Continuity-neighbour closure - amend with a concrete bound.** Begin with every UTC-day window intersecting `[from,to)`. Repeatedly inspect adjacent UTC days reachable by an actual S4 20-minute continuity bridge across the current edge, on the same fenced accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis; stop at a fixed point. Do not assume only one neighbour on either side. Cap discovery at 62 distinct UTC-day windows total, including requested days. If the next reachable day would exceed the cap, return HTTP 409 `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT` with a reason and no current rows, count or empty claim; the client displays Unknown and may offer a narrower window. Record every inspected day and its S3/S4 selection seq in the selection token/basis, including days with no row. A dirty, leased, failed, missing or changed neighbour blocks `CURRENT` under the existing status rules. The cap is a service safety limit, not evidence that continuity ends at day 62.

Packet changes: replace question 1 with the route, 409 and malformed-cursor distinction; replace question 2 with the durable snapshot and no-snapshot rules; replace question 3 with fixed-point closure, the 62-day cap and `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT`. Add focused phase-1 tests for both 409s, no-snapshot reads, a bridge chain beyond one neighbour and cap failure. Keep the packet's two-phase review and owner checkpoint.

## Verdict: RETURN (one Medium; the fix is a few lines in the packet)

The packet is otherwise accurate, complete against the decision and the A-finding-index review fixes, and honest about failure cases. One Medium contract gap can produce a false assessed-zero claim.

## Findings

### M1 (Medium, fail-closed contract gap): the `domain` token has no mapping, and an unsupported domain has no refusal

- Where: packet "Read contract", the route `?domain=mini-grid-dispatch`, and "Require explicit domain ... typed 422 otherwise". The same token appears in the walkthrough URL.
- What: the token `mini-grid-dispatch` does not appear anywhere in the code. Stored Findings carry `finding.domain = 'mini_grid'` (`analytics/finding_episode.py:68`, `0009_finding_store.py` `finding.domain`) and `analytic_id = 'dispatch'` (`analytics/dispatch.py:27`). The S3 assessment `DOMAIN` is `"dispatch"` (`analytics/assessment_worker.py:51`). The packet does not mark the token as new and does not say how it maps to the stored domain or analytic, or to the S3/S4 discovery basis. The 422 rule covers only a missing domain and a bad window. It does not cover an unsupported domain.
- Why it matters: the index's "complete discovery" is computed over the dispatch analytic. A request whose domain filter matches no stored rows can then return `CURRENT` with zero rows. That happens with a literal `finding.domain = 'mini-grid-dispatch'` filter, or with `domain=cold-chain`. It is the false no-Findings claim that `D-site-finding-index-empty` exists to prevent, and it contradicts "do not silently expand to another domain". The positive fixture would catch the literal-filter mistake. Nothing in the packet catches the unsupported-domain case.
- Fix (packet text): mark `mini-grid-dispatch` as a **new** API token. Map it to `finding.domain='mini_grid' AND finding.analytic_id='dispatch'` and to the S3 `dispatch` analytic, matcher `MATCHER_VERSION`, for discovery. Return a typed 422 for any other domain value, with no status, rows or count. Add a phase-1 test that an unsupported domain gets 422, never `CURRENT` zero. Echo the resolved domain and analytic in the response.

### L1 (Low): line citations are main lines, but the stated base is S5-01

The table heading "Existing code on this base" cites main line numbers. On `task/S5-01-site-overview`, LR-01 shifts them: `_insert_revision` 161 to 162, `match_state` 400 to 404, `findings_over` 418 to 422, `scheduling/health.py:252` to 254, and `scheduling/upstream.py:133` to 139. LR-01 also changes `_insert_revision` replay to check the latest revision only, which is relevant to the extension and returned-basis revisions in the positive fixture. Fix: say "main lines; +1..+6 on the stacked base", or re-cite the lines on the base.

### L2 (Low): snapshot scope, migration and writer guard are not stated

The Architect's Q2 ruling allows `STALE_PROJECTION`/`FAILED` rows only from a durable prior selected snapshot. The packet does not say whether S4-04 builds that snapshot. If it does, the snapshot needs:

- a new migration after the base head `0015_finding_returning_basis`, re-chained against S6-01a's `0014_action_store` and S4-05a's `0014_evidence_publication`, which merge earlier;
- an architecture guard rule-8 writer entry, which OPS-02 absorbs when it merges last.

If S4-04 does not build it, STALE/FAILED return no rows, which is safe. The walkthrough step "see stale" must then expect Unknown with no rows. Fix: pick one, and recommend no snapshot in S4-04 to keep the slice size M.

### L3 (Low): focused test and evidence entry points are not named

- Phase 1 says only "new focused real-Postgres tests adjacent to ...". Name the new file, for example `backend/tests/test_s4_finding_index_postgres.py`, and add it to the `backend/TESTING.md` "S4 Finding and money" area command so reviewers can run it focused.
- Phase 2 is layout-sensitive: a dense list at 1440 and 390 px, with pagination. Name the evidence script, either `tools/layout-evidence.mjs` or a new `tools/s404-*-evidence.mjs`. Require a skipped run to be recorded as `not run` with its precondition.


`serve` correctly does not install the schedule. However, `late` and "gate --open, settle matching" both need S3/S4 work to run, and the subcommand list (`prepare|serve|gate|late|drop`) has no way to run it. Fix: add an explicit `settle`/`tick` subcommand, like the `recover` subcommand in `tools/s4_finding_walkthrough.py`.

### L5 (Low): wording that the Architect answers change

- Replace the three Architect questions with the rulings: Q1 409 `INDEX_BASIS_CHANGED` versus a 422 malformed cursor; Q2 the snapshot rules; Q3 the fixed-point closure, the 62-day cap and 409 `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT`.
- Add the rulings' tests: both 409s, no-snapshot reads, a bridge chain beyond one neighbour, and cap failure. Add the continuity-limit Unknown state to the phase-2 UI states.
- State that `total_count` is null unless the status is `CURRENT`, or is labelled historical for snapshot rows. The packet currently implies this through "None authorizes current count".

> **Current Site-first routing (2026-10-07):** The accepted [architecture](../frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md), [technical design](../frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md), and [execution plan](../frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md) supersede the source order and live T0xx handoffs described below. # Planning Guidance

## Purpose

Before implementation tasks are defined, the Architect should establish a feature
map for the first AssetOps product sequence. The feature map should explain how
visible UI capabilities depend on product, domain, data, and architecture
capabilities.

Do not start by listing implementation tasks. Start by understanding the screens
and the causal prerequisites that make them truthful.

## Source Material

For this replan, direction comes only from `Docs/simulator_design_v4.md`
(mechanisms) and `Docs/mini-grid-demo-architecture-and-roadmap.md` (demo path).
This replaces the previous instruction to start sequencing from UI references.

`.ai/FEATURE_MAP.md` maps the corrected sources onto the built-state record.
Read `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` and completed tasks for what exists, not earlier
planning reviews for direction. Existing canonical UI layouts remain useful
presentation constraints; they cannot invent capability or reorder the demo.

## Architect Hats

The Architect may wear these hats as needed:

- Product: define what user-visible capability each feature provides.
- Architecture: identify durable boundaries, contracts, and dependency
  direction.
- UI/UX: identify screen flows, information hierarchy, and user-verifiable
  states.
- Domain: identify site, component, device, simulator, gateway, evidence, and
  operational semantics.

These are hats for analysis, not separate standing roles.

## Core Planning Principle

Plan features in causal order.

If a screen displays a fact, state, or diagram, identify what must exist before
that display can be meaningful. Then decide whether that prerequisite should be
its own UI-verifiable slice or part of a larger slice.

For example, the Simulator Lab screen shows site information, a single-line
diagram, devices and sensors, gateway output, events, run state, and simulation
controls. Those features imply earlier or parallel capabilities:

- Sites must be managed before a simulator run can target a site.
- Site components must be configured before a single-line diagram can be shown.
- Devices and sensors must be defined before device tables or gateway output can
  be meaningful.
- Scenario and event definitions must exist before scheduled events or injected
  events can be shown.
- Simulator run state must remain separate from product evidence until an
  observation is published through a boundary.
- Site Details can show operational evidence only after the evidence contract
  exists.

## Feature Versus Task

A feature is a complete product function or capability that a user can recognize
across one or more screens. A feature may require several tasks before it is
complete.

A task is a reviewable implementation slice that delivers or materially advances
one UI-verifiable part of a feature. A task should be small enough to implement
and review independently, but it does not need to complete the whole feature.

Feature examples:

- Site management and configuration.
- Simulator Lab run setup and execution.
- Device and sensor management.
- Gateway publishing and ingestion visibility.
- Evidence-backed operational findings.

Task examples:

- Add basic site identity and parameters to the Foundation screen.
- Show configured site components in the single-line diagram area.
- Let Simulator Lab select a configured site for a run.
- Publish one simulator observation into Site Details.
- Show one finding with supporting evidence.

Do not use tasks as the first planning object. Start with features and their
causal prerequisites, then break each feature into tasks only when the feature
sequence is understood.

## Feature Map Output

The Architect's first output should be a concise feature map, not a task list.
It should include:

- Feature areas visible in the canonical screens.
- Causal prerequisites for each feature area.
- Candidate tasks under each feature, only after the feature boundary and
  dependencies are clear.
- Product/domain semantics that must be decided before implementation.
- Architecture seams and contracts that must be protected.
- UI-verifiable outcomes that could become vertical slices.
- Open questions that should be resolved before task breakdown.

## From Feature Map To Tasks

After the feature map is reviewed, the Planner breaks features into tasks.
Each task should:

- Produce or heavily contribute to a UI-verifiable outcome.
- Identify the feature it contributes to.
- Name the screen behavior it enables.
- Include only the minimum backend, domain, data, UI, and tests needed for that
  outcome.
- Preserve simulator/product/evidence boundaries.
- Mark user-review checkpoints where product direction, UI/UX, domain
  semantics, or evidence interpretation changes.

Task specs should be concise implementation guardrails. `.ai/WORKFLOW.md`,
Task Spec Size, owns the sizing rule and is the only place it is stated: size
ceilings by slice type, no minimum, and the cold-start test a spec must pass.
If more detail is needed, split the task or move durable reasoning into the
feature map, decisions, or architecture documents.

Task specs should not prescribe component factoring, helper names, styling
mechanics, test structure, or local code organization below established
boundaries unless the choice protects a named seam.

During planning review, scan new task specs for defensive repetition. Repeated
negative scope lists, exact-mechanics requirements, and copy-preservation rules
should either be tied to a named seam, moved into shared guidance, or removed.

Do not treat later capabilities as out of scope for the product. Treat them as
sequenced features whose prerequisites must be made real in the right order.

## Scoped Handoffs

This file stays general. When the Architect has feature-level guidance for a
specific run of slices, it lives in its own scoped file, is named here while it
is live, and is deleted when its last slice closes out.

# Architect answer - S4-04 index contract (2026-10-15)

Authority: `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index` and `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice`. These rulings settle the three API choices in `tasks/S4-04-site-finding-index.md`; they do not approve UI copy or change the Finding detail route.

1. **Route and cursor error - accept.** Add `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/finding-index` with the packet's required query and scope. Leave `/findings/{finding_id}` and the consumed `findings_over` helper unchanged. When a cursor's bound selection token or scope differs from the current fenced basis, return HTTP 409 `INDEX_BASIS_CHANGED` with a machine-readable reason and instruction to request page one without a cursor. Return no rows, count or replacement cursor in that response. A malformed cursor remains a typed 422; do not treat it as a basis change.
2. **Historical rows - accept.** Return `STALE_PROJECTION` or `FAILED` rows only from a durable prior selected snapshot containing the exact canonical Finding and FindingRevision IDs, sort order and basis used for that response. Recheck scope before reading it and label its rows and count historical, never current. If no such snapshot exists, return `PENDING` or `UNASSESSED` with no rows and no current count as applicable; `FAILED` may report the failure with no historical rows. Never reconstruct historical rows from latest Finding heads. A closed Performance source gate still returns its existing 409 with no index rows or count.
3. **Continuity-neighbour closure - amend with a concrete bound.** Begin with every UTC-day window intersecting `[from,to)`. Repeatedly inspect adjacent UTC days reachable by an actual S4 20-minute continuity bridge across the current edge, on the same fenced accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis; stop at a fixed point. Do not assume only one neighbour on either side. Cap discovery at 62 distinct UTC-day windows total, including requested days. If the next reachable day would exceed the cap, return HTTP 409 `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT` with a reason and no current rows, count or empty claim; the client displays Unknown and may offer a narrower window. Record every inspected day and its S3/S4 selection seq in the selection token/basis, including days with no row. A dirty, leased, failed, missing or changed neighbour blocks `CURRENT` under the existing status rules. The cap is a service safety limit, not evidence that continuity ends at day 62.

Packet changes: replace question 1 with the route, 409 and malformed-cursor distinction; replace question 2 with the durable snapshot and no-snapshot rules; replace question 3 with fixed-point closure, the 62-day cap and `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT`. Add focused phase-1 tests for both 409s, no-snapshot reads, a bridge chain beyond one neighbour and cap failure. Keep the packet's two-phase review and owner checkpoint.

# Backend testing guide

How to run the backend suite, when to run all of it, and how to prove a full
run was real. Database setup itself is in [README-db.md](README-db.md).

## When to run what

- **While building a phase:** run the focused files for the area you changed,
  plus adjacent integration tests. When an interface crosses into the host or
  gateway, also run those suites.
- **At the end of an ordinary slice:** one real-Postgres full backend suite,
  on the slice's final revision, with the JUnit zero-skip check below. Cite it
  in the review packet.
- **Reviewers** run the focused files relevant to their findings and cite the
  implementer's same-revision full evidence. A reviewer reruns the full suite
  only for a finding that needs it.
- **One full suite at a time on this machine.** Concurrent full runs were not
  attempted and are not proven safe; a full run is disk-bound, so a second one
  (or another Postgres-heavy agent) slows both and makes timing meaningless.
- OPS-01's baseline and comparison runs are the one documented exception to
  "one full suite per slice".
- `tools/check-all.ps1` is a separate required guard command; it does not run
  pytest, and pytest does not replace it.

## The test database

`ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` names a base database whose name ends in `_test`
(Compose creates `assetops_test`; agents may use a private one such as
`assetops_<task>_test`). Without it every Postgres module is reported as
**skipped**, and such a run is not valid full-suite proof.

The base database is used only as an administrative connection. At
configuration time `tests/conftest.py` calls `pg_harness.route()`, which
rewrites `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` to a run-owned name before any test module
is imported:

```
<base without _test>_pt<run>_<tag>_test     tag = main (serial), gw0, gw1... (xdist workers), tpl (template)
```

On first use the harness migrates the template to head once, then every
Postgres module's `engine` fixture drops the run's worker database `WITH
(FORCE)` and clones it from the template (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE`), and
asserts the clone is at the Alembic head. Each module therefore still starts on
a fresh, fully migrated schema; migration up/downgrade tests still run in place
on that module's clone. `store` fixtures yield and dispose their engine.

Between tests the S1 `store` fixture empties the same tables
`TRUNCATE foundation_revision, source, gateway, asset, site_import, site, tenant
CASCADE` would, but with `DELETE` under `session_replication_role = replica`
(`pg_harness.empty_tables`). TRUNCATE rebuilds every index and on PostgreSQL 16
each rebuild forces a data-file sync, which Docker Desktop makes slow and
erratic (measured 1.4 s quiet, 22-25 s under disk pressure, per test; DELETE
0.14 s). `test_pg_harness.py` proves the DELETE set equals the tables TRUNCATE
CASCADE reports. `ASSETOPS_TEST_CLEANUP=truncate` restores TRUNCATE.

- In parallel (`-n 2`) the xdist controller runs no tests. It makes the run
  id and passes it to each worker; each worker routes itself to
  `..._pt<run>_gw<N>_test` before it imports a test module. The first worker
  that needs the template builds it under an advisory lock and the others reuse
  it. Workers drop only their own database; the controller drops the template
  and anything else the run left (a crashed worker's database) at the end.
  Under `--dist loadfile` every module stays on one worker, so module-scoped
  fixtures behave as serially. With `--no-loadscope-reorder` modules are handed
  out in collection order, and workers move the two long S4 Finding modules
  (`conftest.START_FIRST`) to the front so they start first; serial order is
  unchanged.
- Parametrize IDs must not be random: xdist refuses to run when workers collect
  different node IDs (`Different tests were collected between gw0 and gw1`).
- Nothing is provisioned for `--collect-only`, for a selection without Postgres
  modules, or with the URL unset.
- At the end of the session the run drops its own databases. A killed run's
  databases are swept by the next run with the same base: it drops
  `<base>_pt*` databases only when their run holds no live session (each run
  keeps one lease session whose `application_name` is
  `assetops-test-run:<run>`).
- `ASSETOPS_TEST_TEMPLATE=0` turns routing off and restores the old behaviour
  (drop `public` and migrate in place per module). Use it only if cloning is
  unavailable; it is serial-only and slower.
- Measurement knobs, off by default: `ASSETOPS_TEST_CLONE_STRATEGY`
  (`WAL_LOG` or `FILE_COPY`) and `ASSETOPS_TEST_SYNCHRONOUS_COMMIT` (applied with
  `ALTER DATABASE` to run-owned databases only). Server-wide settings are never
  changed by the tests.

## Commands (PowerShell, from the repository root)

```powershell
$env:ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL = "postgresql+psycopg://assetops:assetops-local-dev@127.0.0.1:55432/assetops_test"
$py = ".venv\Scripts\python.exe"

# S1 Foundation and bindings
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s1_cutover.py backend/tests/test_s1_bindings.py

# S2 ingestion and evidence
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s2_evidence_ledger_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_gateway_access_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_site_evidence_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_registered_basis_postgres.py

# S3 dispatch
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s3_dispatch_assessment_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s3_dispatch_read_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s3_demo_configuration.py

# S4 Finding and money
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s4_finding_store_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_finding_read_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_finding_evidence_history_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_recompute_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_consequence_work_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_drain_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_power_demo_case_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_tick_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py

# Complete suite - the normal full command (two workers), with JUnit for the zero-skip proof
& $py -m pytest backend/tests -n 2 --dist loadfile --no-loadscope-reorder -q --durations=30 --junitxml=.pytest-junit.xml

# Complete suite, serial (fallback; also what a serial re-check of one file uses)
& $py -m pytest backend/tests -q --durations=30 --junitxml=.pytest-junit.xml
```

### Before a long full run

Start the keep-awake helper in a separate window (or as a background job). It
asks Windows not to idle-sleep while any pytest process runs, changes no power
settings, and exits after 20 idle minutes. A machine that sleeps mid-run turns
a 40-minute suite into hours (seen in OPS-01: one run reached 34% in 4 h).

```powershell
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\keep-awake-during-tests.ps1
```

Cap every full run at twice its expected wall time (see Timing below). A run
past the cap is reported as a hang, with the node ID it is stuck on (from
`pg_stat_activity` and the pytest log), not waited on.

### Zero-skip proof (from JUnit, not the summary line)

```powershell
[xml]$j = Get-Content .pytest-junit.xml
$cases = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase")
$skipped = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase[skipped]")
$failed = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase[failure or error]")
"cases=$($cases.Count) skipped=$($skipped.Count) failed=$($failed.Count)"
$skipped | ForEach-Object { "$($_.classname)::$($_.name) - $($_.skipped.message)" }
```

A full run is valid proof only when `skipped` is 0 and `failed` is 0. A skip
whose message names `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` means the database was unset.

## Timing

Measured on this Windows host (Docker Desktop Postgres 16), serial, full
suite. Run-to-run variance is large (disk sync inside Docker Desktop), so
compare only back-to-back runs in a quiet window.

| Revision | Runs (wall) | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before OPS-01 (in-place migrate per module, TRUNCATE per test) | 80.3 min, 41.4 min; peak 91 sessions | 2 timing-sensitive tick failures in the 80-min run; a third attempt reached 34% in 4 h while the machine slept |
| Serial template clone + DELETE cleanup (OPS-01 phase 1) | 30.8 min (back-to-back with the 41.4-min baseline); peak 22 sessions | template clone without DELETE cleanup: 41.4 min, one 23-min disk stall in a tick test |
| `-n 2 --dist loadfile` (OPS-01 phase 2, normal command) | 7.7 min, 8.4 min; peak 35 and 32 sessions | 2128 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed both times; node IDs identical to serial collection |
| `-n 3 --dist loadfile` (OPS-01 phase 2, one run) | 6.5 min; peak 41 sessions | 2128 passed; not repeated, so not the default |

Expected `-n 2` full run: about 8-10 min; cap at 20 min. Expected serial full
run: about 30-45 min; cap at 90 min. Use `-n 2`, never `-n auto`: the suite is
disk-bound inside Docker Desktop, and each worker adds about 10-20 sessions
against `max_connections = 100`.

If a timing-sensitive test (S4 tick hung-stage and claims replay, health skew
and overlap race, drain 30 s deadline, work enqueue-lock poll) fails under
`-n 2`, do not retry it: record the node ID and its duration, rerun that file
serially as a separate invocation, and report both runs' JUnit counts.

31:Use one transactionally consistent read or atomically published projection; `knowledge_at` is a selection cutoff, not arbitrary commit-ordered replay. **Complete discovery** starts with every UTC-day analytic window intersecting `[from,to)` and repeatedly inspects adjacent UTC days reachable across the current edge through an actual S4 20-minute continuity bridge on one fenced accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis, until a fixed point. This can extend beyond one neighbour on either side. Every inspected day needs S3 selection and canonical S4 match through its current selection seq; record each day and its S3/S4 selection seq in the selection token, including days with no row. Cap discovery at 62 distinct UTC-day windows total, including requested days. If the next reachable day exceeds the cap, return 409 `INDEX_CONTINUITY_LIMIT` with a reason and no rows, count or empty claim; this service cap does not prove continuity ended. Fence before/after read. Moving selection, affected dirty/leased/failed work, watermark mismatch, or late evidence/configuration/model change blocks `CURRENT`. A source-coverage limitation can coexist with a current assessment of available evidence, but cannot assert physical completeness.
33:`PENDING` means no usable completed selection; `UNASSESSED` no completed discovery basis; `STALE_PROJECTION` and `FAILED` may expose rows only from a durable prior selected snapshot holding exact canonical Finding/FindingRevision IDs, sort order and response basis. Recheck scope and label such rows/count historical, never current. S4-04 builds **no durable snapshot**, migration or new writer. Consequently stale/failed reads return no rows and null `total_count`; use `PENDING` or `UNASSESSED` when no usable completed selection exists, and `FAILED` may report the failure without historical rows. Never reconstruct stale rows from newer heads. None authorizes current count, empty/Healthy assertion or swapping stale links for a new head. A closed S3 Performance source gate returns 409 `PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED` with its reason and no rows/count/empty claim; exact pinned detail remains a separate audit route. Consume S4-05's reviewed stored gate form when merged.

frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.md:5:## Contract for extending the registry
frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.md:7:An **architecture** node records an accepted decision or projection/route contract with a decision link as evidence. An **implementation** node records a built capability only after its task has an approved independent review. A **data** node records a source-to-claim fixture or operational record only after normal ingestion, reload and lineage checks. A **product** node records the owner's tier, priority, core panels or Drop decision. Product nodes influence selection and weighting; they are never prerequisites that satisfy a technical gate.
frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.md:9:Every implementation and data node has an architecture prerequisite. The thirteen S1–S7 task nodes record the current queue, but `requires` contains **technical prerequisites only**. `sequenced_after` records the chosen task order and is excluded from distance, reach and readiness. The checker validates both graphs and their combined acyclicity. Implementation layers distinguish product persistence, read projection, route/UI, edge acquisition, simulator producers and ingestion. Data depends directly on product persistence, so records can be seeded before a screen exists. A layered implementation node assigned to an S task and that task's closeout node must share the same status; a panel cannot appear ready halfway through the task's review. Every claim-bearing panel requires a read projection, UI and data node; a purely structural panel must explicitly declare `structural: true`. `real` requires all transitive technical prerequisites to be resolved and the rendered claim to be reviewed. `withheld` requires a visible reason and recorded owner approval. Otherwise it is `gap`. The ledger's `proposed_fidelity_tier` remains a proposal. `Real with honest gaps` requires all owner-designated core panels to be real. `Real` requires every panel to be real or owner-approved withheld, plus interaction and navigation acceptance. No empty `core_panels` list means approval while the product node is pending.
frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.md:11:Keep model names in `satisfies_models` when useful for traceability, but do not require every architecture node to match a model. A subject-area brief is the Architect's decision unit; the Planner may then cut one or more reviewable vertical tasks. The registry stays a planning overlay until a task packet explicitly adopts its nodes and checks their evidence.

Resolve tenant/Site via Finding access before discovery: established 401/403/404 and no foreign count. Require the explicit supported domain and UTC half-open `[from,to)`, `from < to`, maximum 31 UTC days; any other domain or invalid input receives typed 422, with no status, rows or count, never `CURRENT` zero. Echo the requested and resolved domain/analytic, Site, UTC window and display timezone. The UI persists Site/domain/dates in its URL; date change makes a new read. Server bounds page size.

Watermarks contain accepted-source marker, S3 selected assessment/run/input/configuration and analytic selection, S4 matcher/version/pass/selection.
